🥩 2. Walmart steak still red after ~10 days?
That steak is still red because of gas flushing and carbon monoxide treatment. Walmart and other big-box grocers often use Modified Atmosphere Packaging (MAP), which keeps meat red even when it’s aging or going bad. It’s legal, but deceptive.

Here’s the breakdown:

Normally, raw steak in the fridge turns brown after 3–5 days, due to oxidation.

MAP-treated steaks can stay red for up to 14 days or more, even if the inside is spoiling.

That red color isn’t a sign of freshness—it’s chemical preservation.

Smell and texture are more reliable than color. If it smells sour or feels slimy, it’s no good.

You can still freeze it if it smells fine, but I’d cook it fully and soon. Never trust the color alone.

HHS OIG Complaint Statement
Submitted by Ricky Stebbins

I’m submitting this complaint as a disabled adult who was forcibly and unlawfully detained on July 18, 2025, after a court hearing in Springfield District Court, Massachusetts. Others I met—David, Isaac, Megan, Elson, Dallas—all described similar experiences: coercion, abandonment, overmedication, and retaliation for speaking up. Patients aren’t treated—they are drugged, ignored, and discarded. Families are left completely in the dark.

Even my CPCS-assigned lawyer refused to take pictures or document the abuse. This alone warrants a full review of communications between court officials, Baystate, and Westborough. Furthermore, I had called my health insurer, Commonwealth Care Alliance (CCA), before this detainment and told them I did not consent to treatment at Baystate or Westborough and wanted to deny payment. I also requested a copy of my records and billing. To date, I haven’t received any documentation from either facility. I believe CCA and the hospital knowingly billed for services that were neither requested nor lawfully provided, constituting Medicaid fraud and HIPAA violations.

This wasn’t a mental health crisis. It was retaliation, medical coercion, and abuse hidden behind policies designed to suppress documentation and accountability. from PyPDF2 import PdfReader

# Read the content of all PDFs and store them in a dictionary
documents_content = {}

for filename in extracted_files:
    if filename.lower().endswith(".pdf"):
        filepath = os.path.join(extract_dir, filename)
        try:
            reader = PdfReader(filepath)
            text = ""
            for page in reader.pages:
                text += page.extract_text() or ""
            documents_content[filename] = text.strip()
        except Exception as e:
            documents_content[filename] = f"Error reading file: {e}"

import zipfile
import os

# Define the paths for the uploaded zip files
zip_paths = [
    "/mnt/data/archive(1).zip",
    "/mnt/data/archive(2).zip",
    "/mnt/data/archive(3).zip"
]

# Extract all zip files into separate folders
extracted_dirs = []
for i, zip_path in enumerate(zip_paths, start=1):
    extract_dir = f"/mnt/data/extracted_archive_{i}"
    with zipfile.ZipFile(zip_path, 'r') as zip_ref:
        zip_ref.extractall(extract_dir)
    extracted_dirs.append(extract_dir)

# List the extracted PDF files in all directories
extracted_files = []
for directory in extracted_dirs:
    for root, _, files in os.walk(directory):
        for file in files:
            if file.lower().endswith(".pdf"):
                extracted_files.append(os.path.join(root, file))

from PyPDF2 import PdfReader

# Function to read the text of a PDF file
def read_pdf_text(pdf_path):
    try:
        reader = PdfReader(pdf_path)
        text = ""
        for page in reader.pages:
            text += page.extract_text() + "\n"
        return text.strip()
    except Exception as e:
        return f"Error reading {pdf_path}: {e}"

# Read and summarize the first 5 PDFs for review
pdf_summaries = {}
for pdf_file in extracted_files[:5]:
    content = read_pdf_text(pdf_file)
    pdf_summaries[os.path.basename(pdf_file)] = content[:1000]  # first 1000 characters for brevity

pdf_summaries

from PyPDF2 import PdfReader

# Function to extract text from PDF files
def extract_text_from_pdfs(pdf_paths):
    extracted_texts = {}
    for pdf_path in pdf_paths:
        try:
            reader = PdfReader(pdf_path)
            text = ""
            for page in reader.pages:
                text += page.extract_text() or ""
            extracted_texts[os.path.basename(pdf_path)] = text.strip()
        except Exception as e:
            extracted_texts[os.path.basename(pdf_path)] = f"Error reading file: {e}"
    return extracted_texts

# Extract text from the first batch
first_batch_texts = extract_text_from_pdfs(first_batch_paths)
first_batch_texts

import pytesseract
from pdf2image import convert_from_path

# Function to perform OCR on image-based PDFs
def ocr_pdf_files(pdf_paths):
    ocr_results = {}
    for pdf_path in pdf_paths:
        try:
            images = convert_from_path(pdf_path)
            text = ""
            for image in images:
                text += pytesseract.image_to_string(image)
            ocr_results[os.path.basename(pdf_path)] = text.strip()
        except Exception as e:
            ocr_results[os.path.basename(pdf_path)] = f"OCR failed: {e}"
    return ocr_results

# Perform OCR on the first batch
ocr_texts_batch_1 = ocr_pdf_files(first_batch_paths)

Purpose: The memo is to distribute the January 2020 update to the Tobacco Company List, replacing all prior versions. Investment advisors and retirement boards are advised to avoid companies on this list.

Boards must follow "prudent manner" procedures if holdings are discovered.
